I'll now produce final answer with reasoning.

I'll produce final answer as: \boxed{(13-11) \times (6 \times (1+1))}.</think>We can start by noticing that 13 – 11 equals 2. Then, if we can get 12 from the other two numbers, 6 and 1, we have 2 × 12 = 24. One way to get 12 from 6 and 1 is to “double” 6. Since 1 + 1 = 2, we can write 6 × (1 + 1) = 6 × 2 = 12. Now, multiplying the two parts gives

  (13 – 11) × (6 × (1 + 1)) = 2 × 12 = 24.

Thus, the final answer is

\boxed{(13-11) \times (6 \times (1+1))}
